§ 16-701 LICENSE ISSUED; INSPECTION; FEE.
   Lifetime bicycle licenses shall be issued through the Police Department at
no cost. Prior to the issuance of the license, the bicycle may be inspected by
the Police Department, and if not in a safe mechanical condition, shall not be
licensed until the same has been repaired.
(Ord. 1032, passed 9-14-1981; Ord. 1208, passed 12-16-1996; Ord. 1444, passed
3-2-2026)
§ 16-702 TRAFFIC REGULATIONS APPLY.
   Any person who rides a bicycle upon any street or alley shall have all of
the rights and shall be subject to all of the duties applicable to the driver
of a vehicle under this chapter, except for those provisions which by their
nature can have no application.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978; Ord. 1444, passed 3-2-2026)
§ 16-703 PARENTAL DUTIES; CHILD LESS THAN 16.
   The parent or guardian of any child, who is less than 16 years old, shall
not knowingly permit any such child to violate any of the provisions of this
article.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978; Ord. 1444, passed 3-2-2026)
§ 16-704 RIDING REGULATIONS.
   The following regulations for the operation and riding of bicycles within
the City are hereby adopted.
   (1)   Any person who rides a bicycle shall not ride other than upon or
astride a permanent and regular seat attached thereto.
   (2)   Any person who rides a bicycle shall not remove his or her feet from
the pedals and shall have at least one hand on the handlebars at all times.
   (3)   Any person who operates a bicycle shall not carry any package, bundle,
or article which prevents the operator from keeping at least one hand upon the
handlebars.
   (4)   No bicycle shall be used to carry more persons at one time than the
number for which it shall be designed and equipped.
   (5)   Any person who operates a bicycle upon a street shall ride as near to
the right side of the street as practicable, exercising due care when passing a
standing vehicle or one proceeding in the same direction.
   (6)   Any person who rides upon any bicycle, coaster, roller skates, sled,
skis, or toy vehicle shall not attach such or himself or herself to any vehicle
upon a street.
   (7)   Any person who rides a bicycle upon a street shall not ride more than
single file except on paths or parts of streets set aside for the exclusive use
of bicycles.
   (8)   Whenever a usable path for bicycles has been provided adjacent to a
street, bicycle riders shall use the path and shall not use the street.
   (9)   Any bicycle when in use at nighttime shall be equipped with a light on
the front which shall emit a white light visible from a distance of at least
500 feet to the front on a clear night and with a red reflector on the rear
which shall be visible on a clear night from all distances between 100 feet and
600 feet to the rear when directly in front of lawful lower beams of headlights
on a motor vehicle. A light emitting a red light visible from a distance of 500
feet to the rear may be used in addition to the red reflector.
   (10)   Any bicycle used on a highway shall be equipped with a brake which
will enable the operator to make the braked wheel skid on dry, level, and clean
pavement.
   (11)   No person shall operate a bicycle at a speed greater than is
reasonable and prudent under the conditions then existing.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978; Ord. 1133, passed 11-5-1990; Ord. 1444, passed 3-
2-2026) Penalty, see §
16-705
§ 16-705 VIOLATION; PENALTY.
   Any person, firm, or corporation violating the terms of this article shall
be subject to the blanket penalty of §
18-104. Each day that the violation is committed or permitted to continue may
constitute a separate offense and may be punishable as such hereunder.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978; Ord. 1444, passed 3-2-2026)

§ 17-101 WATER SERVICE RULES AND REGULATIONS PART OF CONSUMER’S CONTRACT;
SERVICE DEPOSIT.
   The rules, regulations, and water rates as set out in this chapter and as
adopted by the City Council shall be considered a part of the contract with
every person, persons, company, or corporation who is supplied with water
through the waterworks system of the City, and every such person, persons,
company, or corporation, by receiving and using the water, shall be considered
and held to consent to be bound thereby, and should any such rules or
regulations be violated, the water service shall be terminated to the building
or place of the violation, upon the notice as is provided by law, and shall not
be turned on again until all expenses of shutting off and turning on the water
service, together with all outstanding accounts, service fees, and charges, are
paid in full.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
§ 17-102 APPLICATION FOR.
   Each person, firm, or corporation desiring water service must make
application, upon blanks to be furnished by the City for that purpose. Every
applicant shall accompany the application with a service deposit, when
required, as fixed in this chapter, payable in advance; and where applicant’s
service pipe is to be connected with the City main, the tap fee as fixed herein
shall also be paid in advance.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
§ 17-103 WATER SOLD BY METER MEASUREMENT ONLY; STANDARD METERS REQUIRED;
FURNISHED BY CITY; METERS TEST, REPAIR, AND REPLACEMENT.
   All water furnished, except for certain purposes hereinafter specified, will
be furnished at meter rates, through a meter, and all water meters installed in
connection with the waterworks system of the City shall be furnished by the
City upon payment of the tap fee as hereinafter fixed. All meters so set and
installed will be tested and kept in repair at the expense of the City unless
the damage done was caused by the negligence, willful conduct, or act of
consumer. It shall be the duty of all consumers and licensed plumbers to report
to the Utilities Department any damage to water meters by them found to exist
within 24 hours after the damage has been found, together with the cause
thereof. It shall be unlawful for consumers or licensed plumbers to disconnect
water meters from service lines or otherwise interfere therewith without first
so notifying the Utilities Department. Where a water meter has been installed,
delivery to the consumer shall conclusively be presumed to have been made when
the water passes through the meter and all water shall be paid for once having
passed the meter, irrespective of how used or applied and so lost by leaks or
because of other reasons or on account of its use by any person.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978) Penalty, see §
17-401
§ 17-104 RATE SCHEDULE.
   Each consumer connected to the waterworks system of the City shall be liable
and pay for all water used and consumed. All bills shall designate the number
of cubic feet for the period covered by the bill rendered; and the amount of
the charge therefor shall be computed in dollars and cents. The rate for such
water consumption is hereby established by the fee ordinance.
(Ord. 1027, passed 7-20-1981; Ord. 1049, passed 11-21-1983; Ord. 1235, passed
5-4-1998; Ord. 1300, passed 9-4-2007; Ord. 1317, passed 9-2-2008)
Statutory reference:
   Related state law provisions, see Neb. RS 16-681 and 16-682
§ 17-105 MINIMUM CHARGE.
   There is hereby established a minimum charge, or base unit fee, for water
service to each customer, and the same is established by the fee ordinance.
(Ord. 1027, passed 7-20-1981; Ord. 1049, passed 11-21-1983; Ord. 1300, passed
9-4-2007; Ord. 1317, passed 9-2-2008)
Statutory reference:
   Related state law provisions, see Neb. RS 16-681 and 16-682
§ 17-106 FEES FOR ADDITIONAL SERVICES.
   Fees for additional services may be established by the City Council through
the fee ordinance.
(Ord. 1027, passed 7-20-1981; Ord. 1039, passed 7-26-1982; Ord. 1201, passed 6-
24-1996; Ord. 1317, passed 9-2-2008)
§ 17-107 RATE APPLICATION TO MULTIPLE USE BUILDINGS.
   Payment for each residential living or commercial unit in a multiple use
building having one water service and one water meter shall be based on the
minimum rate. If metered consumption within the building exceeds the volume
provided for in the minimum charge times the number of living and/or commercial
units in the building, the coverage shall be paid for at the established rate.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
§ 17-108 MULTIPLE USE DEFINED.
   Residential living units shall be considered multiple use for residential
living when more than one dwelling unit exists. A dwelling unit is one or more
rooms, designed, occupied, or intended for occupancy as a separate living
quarter, with cooking, sleeping, and sanitary facilities provided within the
dwelling unit for the exclusive use of a single family maintaining a household.
Multiple use for commercial purposes shall be determined by the separate
control or occupancy of commercial areas or distinct businesses established
under separate ownership or management.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
§ 17-109 MULTIPLE UNIT; VACANCY.
   The occupancy or vacancy of a unit, whether commercial or residential, shall
not diminish or relieve the owner for the payment of the rates established.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
§ 17-110 RATES; MOBILE HOMES; MOBILE PARKS.
   Unless separately metered by the City, each mobile home consumer located in
a mobile home park, receiving City water through the mobile home park meter,
shall be liable and shall pay for the water service, the charges to be billed
each month to the mobile home park owner, who shall be further liable and shall
pay for any additional water used over and above the minimum volume times the
number of mobile homes using the water service within the mobile home park, as
shown by the mobile home park meter.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
§ 17-111 BILLS; WHEN DUE; WHEN DELINQUENT; EFFECT OF DELINQUENCY.
   All moneys due the City for water furnished under this chapter will become
due and payable monthly on the fifteenth day of the month succeeding the
period, and shall be delinquent on the sixteenth of the month succeeding said
period and subject to a late fee as established by the fee ordinance. If moneys
due the City are not paid before the delinquent date as specified, the
Utilities Department shall forward to the consumer a disconnect notice within
20 days of such delinquency, and if the bill is not corrected within a ten-day
period thereafter, the water service to such user or properly may be
discontinued until the charges are paid in full.
(Ord. 1303, passed 9-17-2007; Ord. 1317, passed 9-2-2008)
Statutory reference:
   Related state law provisions, see Neb. RS 16-681 and 16-682
§ 17-112 SERVICE SHUT OFF AFTER DELINQUENT DATE; FEE FOR SHUTTING OFF; TURNING
ON.
   In the event the bill is not paid before the same is delinquent as specified
in §
17-111, the Water Department shall forward to the consumer a disconnect notice
within 20 days of such delinquency, and if the bill is not corrected within a
ten-day period thereafter, the water service shall be shut off. Should the
consumer desire to have the water service turned on thereafter, the water bill
must be paid in full, and a service fee, as established by the fee ordinance
shall be charged for the shutting off and turning on of the water.
(Ord. 1201, passed 6-24-1996; Ord. 1303, passed 9-17-2007; Ord. 1317, passed 9-
2-2008)
Statutory reference:
   Related state law provisions, see Neb. RS 16-681 and 16-682
§ 17-113 DISCONNECTION FOR LATE PAYMENT.
   (1)   It is the policy of the City to discontinue utility service to
customers by reason of non-payment of bills only after notice and a meaningful
opportunity to be heard on disputed bills. The City’s form for application for
utility service and all bills shall contain, in addition to the title, address,
room number, and telephone number of the official in charge of billing, clearly
visible and easily readable provisions to the effect:
      (a)   That all bills are due and payable on or before the date set forth
on the bill;
      (b)   That if any bill is not paid by or before that date, a disconnect
notice will be mailed containing a cut-off notice that if the bill is not paid
within 20 days of the mailing of the disconnected notice, service will be
discontinued for non-payment; and
      (c)   That any customer disputing the correctness of his or her bill
shall have a right to a hearing at which time he or she may be represented in
person and by counsel or any other person of his or her choosing and may
present orally or in writing his or her complaint and contentions to the City
Manager. This official shall be authorized to order that the customer’s service
not be discontinued and shall have the authority to make a final determination
of the customer’s complaint.
   (2)   Requests for delays or waiver of payment may be entertained. Questions
of proper and correct billing will be considered. In the absence of payment of
the bill rendered or resort to the hearing procedure provided herein, service
will be discontinued at the time specified, but in no event until the charges
have been due and unpaid for at least 30 days.
   (3)   When it becomes necessary for the City to discontinue utility service
to a customer for non-payment of bills, service will be reinstated only after
all bills for service then due have been paid, along with a turn-on charge in
an amount set by City Council.
(Ord. 1334, passed 6-21-2010)
§ 17-114 DELINQUENT RENTS; LIEN ON PROPERTY.
   All water rates, taxes, or rents, when delinquent, shall be a lien upon the
premises or real estate upon or for which the same is used or supplied. Any
delinquent water rental which remains unpaid for a period of three months after
which they became due may be, by resolution of the City Council, assessed
against the real estate as a special assessment, which the special assessment
shall be certified by the City Clerk to the County Clerk, to be placed on the
tax rolls for collection, subject to the same penalties and to be collected in
like manner as other City taxes, all as provided in Neb. RS 16-682.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
§ 17-115 ACCESS TO METERS.
   The owner or tenant shall provide ready and convenient access to the meter
so that it may be easily examined and read by the Utilities Department, and all
consumers of water shall permit the Utilities Department at all hours between
8:00 a.m. and 4:00 p.m. to enter the premises or building for the purpose of
reading or testing any meter or to examine the pipes, meters, or other
fixtures.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978) Penalty, see §
17-401
§ 17-116 METER OUT OF REPAIR; RENT; HOW COMPUTED.
   Should any meter become out of order or repair and fail to register
properly, the consumer will be charged at the average monthly use as shown by
the meter reading when in good order for six months previous, or fraction
thereof, if the same has not been used that long.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
§ 17-117 RIDGEVIEW COUNTRY CLUB; UNTREATED WATER; RATES; CONDITIONS.
   (1)   Untreated water from the eight-inch City transmission line connected
to the south City water reservoir will be supplied to the Ridgeview Country
Club under the following terms and conditions.
   (2)   The rate shall be established by the fee ordinance, billed monthly,
and paid monthly by Ridgeview Country Club.
(Ord. 1153, passed 8-17-1992; Ord. 1397, passed 9-5-2017)
§ 17-118 BACKFLOW PREVENTION.
   Since the Plumbing Code adopted by the City required the installation of
backflow prevention devices on certain types of plumbing fixtures, equipment,
or services to prevent contamination from non-potable liquids, solids, or gases
being introduced into the potable water supply through cross-connections or any
other piping connections to the system, all City water customers having the
backflow prevention devices, equipped with a test port, shall certify to the
City Utilities Department on a form provided and available at the Office of the
City Utilities Department at City Hall, at least one time annually that the
backflow prevention device has been tested by a Nebraska Department of Health
Grade VI Certified Water Operator; provided, however, that the above and
foregoing does not apply to lawn sprinkling systems, with the exception of
those with provisions to inject toxic substances including lawn chemicals.
(Ord. 1168, passed 5-17-1993; Ord. 1275, passed 3-7-2005)
§ 17-119 CUSTOMER SURVEY.
   All customers of the City water distribution and supply system are required
to do all things on their own premises which may be necessary for the
protection of the public health and safety as determined by the City Utilities
Director, and at least one time every five years shall report all potential
backflow and backsiphonage hazards on their own premises, to the City Utilities
Department on a survey form provided by the City Utilities Department.
(Ord. 1168, passed 5-17-1993)
§ 17-120 FRANCHISE FEE.
   There is hereby assessed a franchise fee to the City for the distribution of
water for sale in an amount established by the fee ordinance based upon the
gross operating revenues which are hereby defined to mean all amounts of money
which the Department receives or becomes lawfully entitled to receive (whether
collected or not) derived from the business of distributing and selling water.
Payment shall be made to the City Treasurer on a monthly basis.
(Ord. 1404, passed 9-4-2018)
ARTICLE 2: CONNECTIONS
Section
   
17-201   Water tap service provided

§ 17-201 WATER TAP SERVICE PROVIDED.
   For the tap fee, the City Utilities Department shall tap the City main to
which applicant’s service pipe will be joined and will insert corporation cock
and gooseneck therein. Applicant shall furnish stop box, ferrules or clamps,
trench, and lay pipe from the City main to stopcock or meter pit. The City
Utilities Department shall install the meter and yoke. The applicant shall
furnish, at his or her own expense, a meter pit and lid to specifications
prescribed by the City Utilities Department, if a meter pit is used, and all
pipe necessary to transport the water beyond the stopcock or meter pit to the
premises to be supplied, and all labor necessary to install the same properly,
which shall be done by a plumber licensed to do the work within the City. Each
consumer shall be responsible for the maintenance and repair of all water lines
and equipment from the City water main to their premises after original
installation, except repair or replacement of water meters.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
§ 17-202 SERVICE PIPE; DEPTH.
   All service pipes must be laid as much under the surface of the ground as
the main pipe in the street, and in no case less than four and one-half feet in
depth, and in all cases be so protected as to prevent rupture from freezing.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978) Penalty, see §
17-401
§ 17-203 STOPCOCKS; STOP BOXES; WHERE PLACE; DUTY OF CUSTOMER; KEEP EQUIPMENT
IN REPAIR.
   Unless otherwise permitted, stopcocks shall be placed in the service pipe at
a uniform distance inside and near the curb line, and protected by a box or
iron pipe, reaching from the top of the stopcock to the surface of the ground,
or suitable size to admit a stop key for turning on and off the stop; and with
cast-iron cover having the word “Water” or the letter “W” marked thereon,
visible and even with the surface of the ground, the stop box or iron pipe to
be kept closed and in good repair. In case of refusal of any consumer to keep
his or her stop box, service lines, or any other equipment on premises up to
meter in good condition, the Utilities Department shall either shut off the
water or may, when directed by the City Manager, put the same in good order, at
the expense of the owner of the premises. Curb stops shall be located two feet
from the street edge of the sidewalk in cases where the water main runs on the
same side of the street; otherwise shall be so located two feet on the property
side of the curb line. Stop boxes may be constructed of galvanized iron pipe of
a suitable diameter with a safe and secure cap.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
§ 17-204 WATER METERS; INSTALLATION, DAMAGE, AND RESPONSIBILITY.
   The City Utilities Department or licensed plumber will install, repair, and
replace water meters, however, the consumer shall be liable for the cost of
repair or replacement of his or her water meter if the damage to same was
caused by his or her lack of ordinary care, or his or her negligence or willful
act. All water meters, whether in meter pits or otherwise, shall be installed
in a horizontal position so that the same may be read easily by employees of
the Utilities Department. Any meter now installed, or hereafter to be
installed, which is difficult or inconvenient to read by reason of the method
of its installation, in the opinion of the Superintendent of the Utilities
Department, shall be reinstalled according to the specifications of the
Utilities Department by the consumer within 20 days after the receipt of
written notice to do so, from the Utilities Department, and if the consumer
refuses or neglects to do so, then the Utilities Department shall re-install
the meter and the cost thereof shall be charged to and paid by the consumer as
water rent. Each consumer receiving water service from the City, whose service
line is not now equipped with a water meter, shall provide a place approved by
the Utilities Department for the installation of the same, either by meter pit
or otherwise, at consumer’s expense, if any, within 20 days after receipt of
written notice to do so from the Utilities Department, and the consumer shall
also provide the auxiliary equipment for the installation of the meter as shall
be specified by the Utilities Department in the notice. Should any consumer
refuse or neglect to supply the place of installation or auxiliary equipment as
required, the Utilities Department is hereby authorized and empowered to
discontinue water service to the consumer immediately and without further
notice and the water service shall not be resumed until the consumer has
complied fully with the requirements, and then only upon payment of the
resumption fee, in addition to the other costs.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
§ 17-205 REPORTS BY PLUMBER REQUIRED.
   Within 48 hours after the completion of any attachment, connection, or
disconnection, the plumber shall make a full report to the Utilities Department
with a description of all the apparatus and arrangements for using the water,
together with a plat showing the exact location by measurement from lot lines,
of stopcock, meter, and service pipes and any further particulars that may be
called for by the Utilities Department. The water will not be turned into any
service pipe until the complete reports are made by the plumber to the
Utilities Department.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
§ 17-206 PERMIT REQUIRED FOR EACH WATER JOB.
   No construction, modification, or repair of any water service shall be
commenced unless the owner or his or her agent shall obtain a permit therefor,
as required by the Plumbing Code and City plumbing regulations.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978) Penalty, see §
17-401
§ 17-207 BARRICADES AND LIGHTS.
   No person shall leave any excavation made in a street, alley or public way,
open at any time without suitable barricades which meet the most current
“Manual on Uniform Traffic Control Devices” specifications, and during the
night warning lights must be maintained at the excavation.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978) Penalty, see §
17-401
§ 17-208 FILLING EXCAVATIONS.
   After service pipes are laid, the opening made in any street, alley, or any
other public ground shall be filled as provided in §§
13-105 and
13-106.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978) Penalty, see §
17-401

Section
   
17-301   Water supply; fire mains; business

§ 17-301 WATER SUPPLY; FIRE MAINS; BUSINESS.
   Proprietors of business establishments will be permitted to connect the
places by larger pipes, with hydrants and hose couplings, with the water mains,
at their own expense, upon application to the Utilities Department and under
the supervision of the Utilities Superintendent, and will be allowed to use the
water for fire purposes only. The proprietor making the connection shall pay a
fire hydrant rental fee, the same as is provided in §
17-106.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
§ 17-302 WATER SUPPLY; FIRE HYDRANT USE.
   All hydrants provided for the purpose of extinguishing fire are hereby
declared to be public hydrants, and no person other than members of the Fire
Department, and then only for the use and purpose of the Department, or persons
specially authorized by the Utilities Superintendent, shall draw water from the
same.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978) Penalty, see §
17-401
§ 17-303 WATER; SPRINKLING SUSPENSION.
   The City reserves the right to suspend the use of water for sprinkling
yards, lawns, gardens, or for irrigation purposes, whenever the City Council
deems it necessary to conserve the water supply.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
§ 17-304 WATER SUPPLY; UNLAWFUL TO SWIM OR BOAT; EXCEPTION; CONTAMINATE.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to swim or boat in any City reservoir,
except by permission of the City Council established by a duly enacted
resolution, or place in or near or around the reservoir, catch basins, or any
other part of the waterworks system of this City, or to place any dirt, filth,
or impure substance whatsoever, or any substance or fluid by which the water
shall be rendered impure, unpotable, or dangerous for human or animal
consumption. Boating is restricted to electric motor or non-powered boats.
(Ord. 1134, passed 2-18-1991; Ord. 1334, passed 6-21-2010) Penalty, see §
17-401
§ 17-305 WATER SUPPLY; RESERVATIONS; LIABILITY.
   The City reserves the right at all times to shut off the water for necessary
repairs or extensions and shall in no manner be liable for any damages caused
by shutting off the supply of water of any consumer while the system or any
part thereof is undergoing repairs or extensions, or caused by the freezing of
the main or the breaking of any pipe or service cock, or by a shortage of water
due to accident by circumstances over which the City has no control, or to an
act of God.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
§ 17-306 UTILITIES SUPERINTENDENT REPORT.
   The Utilities Superintendent shall make a monthly report to the City Council
of all Utilities Department activity for the previous month.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
§ 17-307 WATER SYSTEM PROPERTY; DESTRUCTION OF OR INJURY TO.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to willfully or carelessly break, injure
or deface, interfere with, or disturb any machinery, apparatus, fixtures,
attachment, or appurtenances of the waterworks system of the City, or any
public or private hydrant, hose, stopcock, meter, water supply or service pipe,
or any part thereof, or to deposit anything in any stopcock box, or commit any
act tending to obstruct or impair the intended use of any of the above
mentioned properties.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978) Penalty, see §
17-401
§ 17-308 WATER SUPPLY; SODIUM FLUORIDE OR SODIUM SILCO FLUORIDE; PROHIBITED BY
ELECTION.
   As the result of an election of the people of the City, held 11-4-2008, the
addition or introduction of any sodium fluoride or sodium silco fluoride into
the municipal water supply of the City shall be prohibited.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978) Penalty, see §
17-401
ARTICLE 4: VIOLATION; PENALTY
Section
   
17-401   Violation; penalty
§ 17-401 VIOLATION; PENALTY.
   Any person, firm, or corporation violating the terms of this chapter shall
be subject to the blanket penalty of §
18-104. Each day that the violation is committed or permitted to continue may
constitute a separate offense and may be punishable as such hereunder.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
CHAPTER 18: GENERAL PROVISIONS
   Article
      
1.   GENERAL PROVISIONS
ARTICLE 1: GENERAL PROVISIONS
Section
   
18-101   Adoption; repeal of prior ordinances in conflict
   
18-102   Repeal; exceptions
   
18-103   Validity
   
18-104   Blanket penalty
   
18-105   Purpose of titles
   
18-106   Amendment; revision
   
18-107   Effective date
§ 18-101 ADOPTION; REPEAL OF PRIOR ORDINANCES IN CONFLICT.
   This code, embracing all the ordinances of the City, as revised and prepared
under the authority of the City Council and now presented to the City Council
for adoption, be and the same is hereby adopted and declared to be the
ordinances of the City, and the municipal code, as classified into chapters as
set forth in this code, now in force from and after publication of the same in
pamphlet form. General Revision Ordinance 1000 and all ordinances and parts of
ordinances of a general or permanent nature passed and approved thereafter and
prior to the passage and approval of this code, which are in conflict with any
of the provisions of this code, are hereby repealed.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
§ 18-102 REPEAL; EXCEPTIONS.
   The repeal of ordinances, as provided in §
18-101, shall not affect any rights acquired, fines, penalties, forfeitures, or
liabilities incurred thereunder, or actions involving any of the provisions of
any ordinances and part thereof. Any ordinances above repealed are hereby
continued in force and effect after the passage, approval, and publication
hereof for the purpose of all the rights, fines, penalties, forfeitures, and
liabilities and actions thereof.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
§ 18-103 VALIDITY.
   Each chapter, article, section, and subdivision of a section of this code is
hereby declared to be independent of every other chapter, article, section, or
subdivision of a section so far as inducement for the passage of this code is
concerned; and the invalidity of any chapter, article, section, or subdivision
of a section of this code shall not invalidate any other chapter, article,
section, or subdivision of a section thereof.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
§ 18-104 BLANKET PENALTY.
   Any person, firm, or corporation, or their agents or servants, who shall
violate any of the provisions of the municipal code of ordinances, chapters,
articles, sections, or subdivisions of sections included herein, unless
specifically otherwise provided herein, may be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor
and, upon conviction thereof, and when the penalty is not inconsistent with the
general laws of the state, shall be fined in any sum not exceeding $1,000, and
the costs of prosecution. Each day that the violation is committed or permitted
to continue may constitute a separate offense and may be punishable as such
hereunder.
(Ord. 1298, passed 8-20-2007)
Statutory reference:
   Related state law provisions, see Neb. RS 16-246
§ 18-105 PURPOSE OF TITLES.
   The titles appearing in connection with the foregoing chapters, articles,
and sections are inserted simply for convenience, and they shall be wholly
disregarded by any person, officer, court, or other tribunal in construing the
terms and provisions of this municipal code.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
§ 18-106 AMENDMENT; REVISION.
   Any chapter, article, section, or subdivision of a section included in this
municipal code and any other independent ordinance, chapter, article, section,
or subdivision of a section of an ordinance duly enacted by the City Council
shall be altered, amended, or revised only by the complete nullification and
repeal of the ordinance, chapter, article, section, or subdivision of a section
and by the substitution of a new ordinance, chapter, article, section, or
subdivision of a section containing the entire ordinance, chapter, article,
section, or subdivision as amended, altered, or revised.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
§ 18-107 EFFECTIVE DATE.
   This code shall be published in pamphlet form and shall be in full force and
effect from and after its passage, approval, and publication as provided by
law.
(Ord. 1000, passed 12-4-1978)
